  1. Westpac
  1. Term deposit
    • 364.2. there are no documents to verify the accuracy of the net capital gain declared by MZA #1 in its 2011 ITR (i.e., $225,798) and the Applicant’s share of the gross capital gain of $274,360.49 (as reflected in MZA #1’s 2011 Handisoft GL DZZZ.01.55). The claim regarding the capital gain is also not reflected in the 2011 ITR where the Applicant declared no capital gain tax event and nil capital gain.
  2. There are no contemporaneous documents evidencing the Applicant’s unit holdings in MZA #1.
  3. The Commissioner notes the following inconsistent information provided by the Applicant and her advisers:
  • 366.1. Mr D stated this term deposit was in the name of MZA #1 and so cannot be considered as income,[154] whereas during objection Mr G advised that the capital distributed to the Applicant by MZA #1 was derived from the sale of the two properties (the North Rocks property and the Chippendale property);[155]
  • 366.2. In the Applicant’s response dated 20 June 2018, the Applicant stated that the capital gains distributed by the MZA Trust was derived from the sale of properties of the North Rocks property and the Chippendale property:[156]
    “$183,614.75 was debited in the MZA Trust’s 2011 Handisoft GL CAGD (bank loans) to record the repayment of the loan. This was funded from the proceeds of the sale of (the North Rocks property) and it reconciled to the settlement adjustment worksheet you supplied. The Commissioner notes that in relation to (the Chippendale property) the settlement worksheet showed an amount of $1,078,664.14 was directed to National Australia Bank Limited.[157] However, on 26 August 2010, the amount debited in MZA Trust’s 2011 GL CAGD (bank loans) was only $692,220, not $1,078,664.14 with a description sale of (the street). Further, the settlement date was 26 November 2010, but the transaction was recorded/dated 26 August 2010.”
  • 366.3. Mr G stated the $180,000 formed part of the Applicant’s share of the gross capital gain resulting from the sale of two properties (the North Rocks property and the Chippendale property) by MZA #1. Whereas Mr D stated that the $180,000 was from the redemption of units in MZA #1.[158]
  • 366.4. the Applicant also stated that on 14 February 2011, the $464,000 deposited into her bank account was funded from the proceeds of the sale of the Chippendale property.[159] That deposit was currently being characterised as a unit redemption and not a trust distribution.[160]
  1. At the hearing the Applicant stated:
    [500] “On 14 February 2011 I received a $180,000 payment from MZA #1, this payment is transaction 640 of the Annotated Transactions (Tab 11 page 59).

    [501] The $180,000 payment not recorded by me in MYOB because it was not paid from the MZA #1 bank accounts. This amount was the return of the amount that MZA #1 placed on term deposit with Westpac (term deposit BEG00). I did not keep records of term deposit investments in MYOB.

    a. At T-302 is the Term Deposit Repayment Advice for term deposit BEGZ00, advising that the amount placed on deposit was credited to account GCBC49 FZCD17, which is my Westpac bank account, at the end of the period on which the monies had been placed on deposit.

    [502] This payment was an advance payment of part of my $274,360.49 entitlement to the proceeds of the sale of properties that year by the trust, as detailed from paragraph 449.

    [503] At the end of the 2011 tax year I was owed $79,631.07.

    a. At T-1176 is a copy of the MZA #1 2011 general ledger, account DZZZ.01, my loan account, has a final balance of $79,631.07 owed to me (at T-1183).

    b. At Tab 153 is a copy of the MZA #1 2011 financial statements showing on page 2362 the $79,631.07 amount owed as a financial liability on the balance sheet

    [504] Included in this $180,000 payment is the $49,698 trust distribution declared on my income tax return. The remainder is not taxable income.”

  2. In closing submissions the Commissioner submitted:
    [245] “The Applicant describes this amount as the advance payment of a capital gain to which she was entitled from MZA 1 from the sale of properties by that trust during the income year ended on 30 June 2011 (WS-502 (page 132)).

    [246] The Applicant did not return any capital gains in her 2011 income tax return (T93- T-96 esp T-95) and the revised calculation of the Applicant’s taxable income for that year refers to capital gains from units rather than a capital gain to which the Applicant was specifically entitled (MM-25ff, Tab7).

    [247] The explanation at WS-502 is inconsistent with the actual return and proposed recalculation of the Applicant’s taxable income for the 2011 Year. The explanation does not satisfy the standard of proof required to discharge the burden of proof.”

  3. The evidence available and the various representations made are not consistent and inconclusive. There is no evidence that the representations made by Mr D and Mr G respectively were wrong. Some documentation or contemporaneous recognition (other than journal entries). No other witness was called to verify the Applicant’s claim.

Finding

  1. The Tribunal is not satisfied, on the balance of probabilities, that the undeclared deposit of $180,000 arose from a capital gain distribution made by MZA #1 to the Applicant. The $180,000, therefore, does comprise part of the Applicant’s assessable income.

Amount Described As Interest On Term Deposit: $5,888.22

  1. The Applicant concedes the amount of $5,888.22 described as interest has been correctly assessed as income. The Applicant states it was inadvertently not recorded in the accounts of MZA #1 and was neither declared as income in the trust nor in the 2011 ITR.[161]
  2. This issue is no longer in dispute.

Amount Described As Proceeds From The Sale Of The Beneficial Interest In The TLP To SUT Of $750,000 But An Amount Of $1,000,000 Was Incorrectly Deposited Into Your Bank Account (Overpaid By $250K)

  1. In the 2011 Year, the Applicant received two $1,000,000 deposits on 14 February 2011 from an unidentified source. This amount was not disclosed in the 2011 ITR.
  2. In 2014 the Applicant stated the deposits were from:[162]
    • 374.1. proceeds from the sale of interest in TLP to SUT; and
    • 374.2. redemption of units in the TAT Trust (the payment being funded from the sale of its shares in SMS).
  3. The Commissioner does not accept the Applicant’s explanation because:
    • 375.1. there was no evidence provided to support the sale of her interest in the TLP;
    • 375.2. there was no evidence showing a change in the partnership structure on or around 14 February 2011.
    • 375.3. The TAT Trust is a discretionary trust and did not have the ability to issue units. Therefore, the $1,000,000 deposit on 14 February 2011 could not have been for the redemption of units in the TAT Trust.
  4. At objection the Applicant said:
    • 376.1. the amount deposited resulting from the proceeds of sale of 50% of the beneficial interest in TLP to SUT should have been $750,000 (being half of the offered purchase price, as disclosed in the letter of offer), but an amount of $1,000,000 was incorrectly deposited into her bank account;[163]
    • 376.2. the second $1,000,000 represented a distribution made by the TAT Trust arising from the sale of its interest in SMS, which occurred on 14 February 2011[164] and that the amount distributed should have been $1,250,000 (being the sale of SMS by the TAT Trust).
  • 376.3. there was an error in the allocation of purchase funds between two businesses (i.e., TLP and SMS) but this was of no consequence because the correct total sums were transferred).[165]

“Sale of interest in TLP to SUT” of $1,000,000

  1. The Applicant contends that she disposed of her interest in TLP to SUT and this gave rise to a capital gain. The Worksheets (Part D of the 2011 Worksheet) provided set out how the Applicant calculates the purported capital gain.
  2. At the time the trustee of SUT was Mr WYVW Investment Pty Ltd. On 1 July 2017, the Trustee of SUT changed to DAC Pty Ltd by deed. The change of trustee is not in dispute.[166]
  3. The following documents are relevant to the Applicant’s contention include:
    • 379.1. “Minutes of Resolution” dated 22 September 2010 by Mr WYVW Investments Pty Limited resolving that the offer contained in Letter of Offer should be made. The meeting was constituted by Mr WYVW as Chairman;[167]
    • 379.2. A “Letter of Offer” dated 22 September 2010 made by Mr WYVW Investments Pty Limited (as purchaser) to the Applicant and Mr WYVW (as vendors). The letter states Mr WYVW Investments Pty Limited offered to acquire an “equitable estate” in TLP. The consideration offered for the acquisition is $1,500,000. The offer was stated to be subject to the right of SMS Pty Ltd to benefit from TLP for three months from the date of acceptance of the offer. The letter provided:[168]
    “Mrs WYVW and Mr WYVW may accept the offer contained in this letter by dealing with the promissory note and delivering a power of attorney for the Limited Partnership Interest to SMS Pty Ltd of (the Glebe property) in the state of New South Wales as Custodian.”
  • 379.3. a “Promissory Note” dated 22 September 2010, under which Mr WYVW Investments Pty Limited promises to pay, on demand, the amount of $1,500,000 to the Applicant and Mr WYVW. There is also a promise to pay interest at the rate determined in accordance with the definition of “benchmark interest rate” in section 136 of the Fringe Benefits Tax Assessment Act 1986 (Cth)”;[169]
  • 379.4. a “Power of Attorney”: A power of attorney (POA) granted by the Applicant and Mr WYVW (as Grantors) to Mr WYVW Investments Pty Limited. The POW gave Mr WYVW Investments Pty Limited the power to do anything the Grantors may lawfully authorise an Attorney to do in respect to their interest in TLP. The POW was witnessed by Mr V;[170]
  • 379.5. SUT’s 2012 balance sheet which included an amount of $1,500,000 recorded as “Current Assets - interest in other entities” (constituting SUT’s acquisition of a 50% equitable interest from Mr and Mrs WYVW each for $750,000, totalling to $1,500,000);
  • 379.6. an excerpt of the SUT’s Westpac bank account to show the various cheque withdrawals that occurred on the date of sale of 14 February 2011 as evidence of the payments made to the Applicant.
  1. The Applicant advised there was “no completed sales contract” and said, “the offer was accepted by conduct and the transaction concluded on that basis”.[171]
  2. The Commissioner submits that these documents do not establish that the deposit of $1,000,000 arose from the sale of the Applicant’s beneficial interest in TLP because:
    • 381.1. the Applicant had advised on 3 May 2019 that there was no completed sales contract. There was no formal agreement for the sale and no document corroborating any acceptance of the offer; and
    • 381.2. no CGT event was declared in her 2011 ITR.
  3. The Commissioner also notes that the valuation of TLP was not conducted by a professional valuer and there is a lack of information which would permit the Commissioner to accept the valuation.
  4. The Applicant says no CGT was payable due to the application of small business CGT concession in Division 152 of the ITAA 1997, which reduced her income tax liability to nil. The Commissioner says even if there was a capital gain made, there is insufficient evidence to show the conditions under section 152-10 of the ITAA 1997 were satisfied (in particular, the $6 million net asset value test).
  5. In order to calculate the capital gain, the market value of the units held by the applicant in MZA #1 had to be calculated. The Applicant arranged for the MZA #1 properties to be valued by a registered valuer, Mr W. Mr W is certified by the Australian Property Institute as a “Certified Practicing Valuer”. Mr W valued the Bondi property at $1,940,000.
  6. The Commissioner pointed out this is less than half the value given for one of the properties (the Bondi property) in the letter to Westpac of 10 December 2010. In that letter (the purpose of which was to obtain funding of $4,000,000 to facilitate the 2011 business restructure) the Bondi property is referred to by Mr V as having a value of $4,000,000.[172]
  7. Following receipt of this letter Westpac agreed to provide the funding.
  8. The Applicant submitted the value of the properties listed in the 10 December 2010 letter “as being available for security against any loan appear to be no more than the subjective opinion of the author of the letter as to the value of those properties. It is further noted that a condition of the finance approval by Westpac on 14 February 2011 was that (the Bondi property) be revalued by a Bank Panel Valuer at a minimum market value of $4,000,000.80 There is no evidence that any such valuation was ever undertaken”.
  9. The Applicant did not know if the Bondi property had a market value different to $4,000,000 as at 10 December 2010.[173]
  10. There are serious doubts as to the true value of the Bondi property and therefore the value of the units in the trust.
  11. At the hearing the Applicant provided the following evidence:
    [518] “On 14 February 2011, I received credits into my Westpac account totalling $2,000,000 from the restructuring of the Group business as discussed from paragraph 522. These payments are listed in Part G.9 (Tab 7 page 30). The total amount consisted of:

    a. $750,000 from the sale of my 50% beneficial interest in TLP to SUT for $750,000.

    b. A $1,250,000 capital distribution from TAT Trust being 50% of the proceed of the sale by the trust to SUT of its shares in SMS Pty Ltd.

    [519] On that same day, Mr WYVW also sold his interest in TLP to the WYVW Settlement Unit Trust for $750,000 and received a $1,250,000 payment from TAT Trust, being 50% of the proceed of the sale by the trust to SUT of its shares in SMS Pty Ltd

    [520] At T-511 is a covering letter from Westpac enclosing a list of all the transactions processed by it on 14 February 2011 in connection with the business restructuring. As discussed at paragraph 529, the payments to me were made by way of two $1,000,000 deposits instead of one deposit of $750,000 and one deposit of $1,250,000. These payments are the 5th and 6th transactions listed at T-512.

    [521] The net capital gain on these transactions was calculated as set out in Parts C.1 and C.2 of the 2011 Worksheet. In addition to the general discount, the Active Asset Reduction and retirement concessions were applied to these capital gains, resulting in a nil assessable capital gain.”

  12. The Commissioner pointed out that the bank statements do not assist to identify the character of the payment:[174]
    [93] “On 14 February 2011, the Applicant received two separate deposits of $1m each into a bank account (FZCD17) kept by the Applicant with Westpac.

    [94] Westpac described each deposit (T-512) as from Mr WYVW Investments Pty Ltd as trustee for the SUT and TAS as trustee for the TAT Trust. The latter deposit is the subject of the submissions under heading 4.4.2, immediately below.

    [95] An extract from the Westpac bank statement of SUT (Account BF-CB16) for the period 31 January 2011 to 28 February 2011 (T-456) records four withdrawals on 14 February 2011, each depicted as “withdrawal/cheque ADZB11”. Two of those withdrawals are for $1m. No payee in relation to those withdrawals is identified by the bank statement.

    [96] The character of the payment cannot be determined from these entries. There is no proof, for example, that the receipt is capital proceeds for any CGT event. Further there is no proof that part of either $1m receipt represents capital proceeds because there is no proof of which part, or of the character of the other part.”

  13. At the hearing the Commissioner pointed out:[175]
    • 392.1. there is real doubt about the legal content and effect of the offer where:
      1. The Applicant did not recall when she received the offer contained in the 22 September 2010 letter;[176]
      2. There is an apparent inconsistency between the date recorded on the offer and the existence of the offeror relationship because SUT was not settled until 23 December 2010.[177] The Applicant provided no explanation.
      3. It is unclear what asset the Applicant was purporting to sell because during cross-examination the Applicant said she thought that by signing the power of attorney she would not be disposing of her partnership interest, but rather her “equitable interest, or whatever you would call it, in the TAS finance book...”;[178]
    • 392.2. it is unclear whether there was a CGT event because:
      1. it is unclear whether acceptance preconditions were fulfilled, or when they were fulfilled, and it is unclear when the offer was actually made (the offer was incapable of acceptance before it was made).
      2. the two preconditions stated in the Letter of Offer were:
        • 392.2.2.1. that there be a “dealing” with the promissory note; and
        • 392.2.2.2. the delivery of a power of attorney for the TLP interest to SMS of the Glebe property in the state of New South Wales as Custodian.
      3. There is no evidence of how the promissory note was dealt with – the Applicant could not recall whether she had dealt with or demanded payment, or whether she had received any interest in the discharge of the promissory note.[179]
  14. It is unclear on the evidence whether there was a dealing with the promissory note. During cross-examination of the Applicant the following exchange occurred which demonstrated the unreliability of the Applicant’s evidence:[180]
    1. “Did you deal with the promissory note delivered with the offer?---I don’t recall.
      1. Did you demand payment on the promissory note?---On that date?
      2. That date or at all?---Well, the offer happened in - some months later on 14 February 2011. The sale happened on that date.
      3. Did you demand payment on the promissory note on that date?---I don’t recall.”
  15. There are too many purported “errors” and uncertainties in relation to this transaction which makes it appear implausible.
  16. The Applicant says the errors were made but gives no explanation for why or how those errors were made. No communications with the accountant are before the Tribunal. There is no indication of the advice sought or given nor is there any advice of the instructions provided.
  17. There is no proof of sale of the Bondi property. There is insufficient evidence for this sale before the Tribunal.
  18. In this circumstance, given the complexity of the transactions, the Applicant’s evidence as outlined above is insufficient to persuade the Tribunal. No explanation was given, other than with respect to the Applicant’s former accountant, for why no other witnesses were called to corroborate the Applicant’s evidence.

Amount Described As “Distribution From The TAT Trust Arising From The Sale Of The TAT Trust’s Shares In SMS Of $1,250,000” But Only $1,000,000 Was Deposited Into Your Bank Account (Underpaid By $250K)

  1. The Applicant states the reason why the investment in SMS was not reflected in the TAT Trust’s financial account was due to the error made by the previous accountant.[181]
  2. As a result of the error the Applicant says the 2011 financial accounts of the TAT Trust were drawn to properly account for that asset and its disposal:[182]
    • 399.1. in the amended financials of the TAT Trust, the beneficiary loan of SMS was reduced from $64,394 (FY2010) to nil (FY2011).
  3. The Commissioner notes that a copy of 2006 shareholders agreement indicates shares in SMS were beneficially held by the TAT Trust.[183] Australian Securities and Investment Commission records showed that TLP was the beneficial owner of the SMS shares. The Applicant says the ASIC record is incorrect as TLP never traded on its own and had always only acted as trustee for the TAT Trust.[184]
  4. The Commissioner does not accept the position that the amount of $1,250,000 was a distribution from TAT Trust arising from the sale of the TAT Trust’s shares in SMS and maintains the position that the deposits are properly assessable as income considering that:
    • 401.1. no CGT event was disclosed in the 2011 ITR;
    • 401.2. The Applicant had previously confirmed there were no completed sales contract between the TAT Trust and SMS;[185]
    • 401.3. ASIC records were only updated after the audit. Prior to that the Applicant had maintained TLP beneficially held shares in SMS;[186] and
    • 401.4. there were inconsistencies between some documents and explanations such as in one response to the Commissioner the Applicant stated the $1,000,000 received on 14 February 2011 was related to proceeds received from unit redemption from the TAT Trust. However, on objection the Applicant said it was from proceeds of sale of shares in SMS. Yet the TAT Trust’s 2010 MYOB records do not show it held any shares in SMS.
  5. The Applicant says (see also [374], [376] and [390] above):
    • 402.1. the amount received of $750,000 is the sale proceeds from her interest in TLP. The net capital gain from the sale of the business was calculated and is set out in the 2011 Worksheet; and
    • 402.2. the amount received of $1,250,000 is her share of the sale proceeds from the sale by TAT Trust of all its shares in SMS. The net capital gain from the sale of the business was calculated and is set out in the 2011 Worksheet.
  6. Again, given the complexity of the transactions, the evidence (some of which was inconsistent and unexplained as outlined above), is insufficient to persuade the Tribunal that the amount of $1,000,000 is not income. No explanation for why no other witnesses, except for the accountant, has been given.

Finding

  1. The Tribunal is not satisfied on the balance of probabilities that the undeclared deposits totalling $2,000,000 were:
    • 404.1. proceeds from the sale of interest in TLP to SUT; and
    • 404.2. redemption of units in the TAT Trust.
  2. The deposits of $2,000,000, therefore, comprise part of the Applicant’s assessable income.

An Amount Of $464,000 Described As Proceeds From The Redemption Of Units In MZA #1

  1. In the 2011 Year, the Applicant received a bank deposit on 14 February 2011 from MZA #1 of $464,000. This amount was not disclosed in the 2011 ITR.
  2. In 2014 the Applicant said:[187]
    • 407.1. the deposit was from proceeds from redemption of units in MZA #1 and the payee was MZA Investments Pty Ltd; and
    • 407.2. 2,915,443 units in MZA #1 were acquired on 1 July 2007.
  3. Following the audit, the Commissioner amended the 2011 ITR on the basis that the deposit represented undeclared income.
  4. The Commissioner reached its decision because:
    • 409.1. a lack of appropriate records in accordance with the terms of the MZA Trust deed to demonstrate the actual intention of the parties and true characterisation of the transaction; and
    • 409.2. there was no disclosure in the 2011 ITR of any CGT event arising from the alleged redemption of units in the MZA #1 Trust.
  5. The Commissioner identified the following lack of documents:
    • 410.1. no contemporaneous documents to support the purchase and subsequent sale of units. For example, unit certificates to substantiate the unit holding in the MZA #1 as required under Clause 7 of the MZA #1 Trust Deed and bank statements to evidence the purchase of the units in MZA #1;
    • 410.2. no evidence of the alleged redemption of units in MZA #1. Clause 9 of the Trust Deed requires unit redemptions to be made in writing to the trustee accompanied by the appropriate unit certificate. No documents evidencing cancellation of the unit certificates were provided; and
    • 410.3. no CGT event was declared in the 2011 ITR.
  6. The Commissioner also identified inconsistencies between records and explanations provided as follows:
    • 411.1. MZA #1’s unit register indicates the Applicant redeemed 464,000 units on 25 June 2011, but:
      1. the deposit of $464,000 occurred four months prior to the unit redemption; and
      2. the Applicant told the Commissioner the units were redeemed on 14 February 2011;[188]
    • 411.2. in March 2019 the Applicant told the Commissioner the $464,000 was funded from the proceeds of the sale of the property at the Chippendale property in November 2010,[189] whereas in June 2018 the Applicant treated the deposit as a redemption of units in MZA #1 and contended that the amount of $180,000 in dispute represented a capital gain distribution from MZA #1 arising from the sale of the properties at the North Rocks property and the Chippendale property;
    • 411.3. MZA #1’s 2011 MYOB records showed on 14 February 2011 the $464,000 was recorded as a loan asset of MZA #1 as follows:

“Debit – Account 1-BZHZ ‘Loan to Mrs WYVW’ (Loan asset account) $464,000

Credit – Account 1-AAZA ‘Westpac No 1’ $464,000”

  1. In her witness statement the Applicant stated:
    [148] In the 2011 financial year I redeemed units in MZA #1 Trust to the value of $464,000. This transaction was part of the business restructuring transactions and is discussed further from Part 6 paragraph 522.

    a. At T-1176 is a copy of the MZA #1 2011 general ledger which shows at T-1184 the $464,000 redemption as the only transaction in account DBDZ “Subscribed Units – Mrs WYVW”.

    [149] No other units in MZA #1 were acquired or redeemed by any person or entity in the 2011 financial year.

    a. At T-1184 there are no transactions shown in account DBDZ.10, the WYI Super subscribed units account.

    [150] As calculated at Part D of the MZA #1 Unit Worksheet at Tab 3, the redemption price for these units was $3.37. I redeemed 137,685.05 units, reducing my total unitholding to 1,098,087.68 units.

  2. ...
  3. $464,000 Payment from MZA Trust #1
    [496] On 14 February 2011 $464,000 was deposited into my bank account by Westpac. This was paid from the MZA #1 Westpac bank account. This is transaction 630 of the Annotated Transactions (Tab 11 page 59).
  4. a. At Tab 152 is a copy of the MZA #1 2011 MYOB records. I recorded this payment to me in account no 1-BDHZ “Loan to Mrs WYVW” at page 2347, being my loan account, because it was received prior to the finalisation of the unit redemption.
    [497] At the end of Tax year 2011, this amount was allocated as payment for the redemption of units to that value in MZA #1.

    [498] It was always intended that the $464,000 payment would be for a redemption of units in the trust.

    a. It was identified as such in the transaction instructions prepared for Westpac as referred to in paragraph 529 above. The specific instruction is at T-509.

    b. At T-1176 is a copy of the MZA #1 2011 general ledger recording in account DBDZ “Subscribed Units – Mrs WYVW” (T-1184) a debit reflecting an amount payable to me upon redemption of units to the value of $464,000 by me.

    c. At Tab 153 is a copy of the MZA #1 2011 Financial Statements showing the reduction in the value of units held by me as a result of the redemption of units to $2,164,873 from the $2,628,871 value in the MZA #1 2010 financial statements (Tab 72, page 1288)

    [499] The $84,921.71 net capital gain from this redemption of units has been included as in my calculation of my assessable income. See from paragraph 0. The remainder of the $464,000 payment is a return of subscribed capital and not taxable income

  5. The lack of sufficient records and inconsistent representations made about these transactions such that the Tribunal cannot be satisfied that the deposit was from proceeds from redemption of units in MZA #1.
  6. These transactions relate to entities under the Applicant’s direction and under supervision and guidance of tax accountants and solicitors. It is reasonable to expect that documentation would have been prepared.
  7. No corroborating witnesses were called.

Finding

  1. The Tribunal is not satisfied, on the balance of probabilities, that the undeclared deposit of $464,000 deposit was from proceeds from redemption of units in MZA #1 to the Applicant. The $464,000 deposit, therefore, comprises part of the Applicant’s assessable income.

An Amount Of $645,000 Described As Proceeds From The Redemption Of Units In The LST Trust

  1. In the 2011 Year, the Applicant received a bank deposit on 14 February 2011 from MZA Investments Pty Ltd of $645,000. This amount was not disclosed in the 2011 ITR.
  2. The Applicant says:[190]
    • 418.1. the deposit was from proceeds from redemption of units in the LST Trust and the payee was MZA Investments;
    • 418.2. units in the LST Trust were acquired on 1 July 2007.
  3. The Applicant states this amount was received as payment for the redemption of units in the trust. The Applicant has now calculated a net capital gain of $50,455.37 from the redemption of the units (see 2011 Worksheet[191]).
  4. Following the audit, the Commissioner amended the 2011 ITR on the basis that the deposit represented undeclared income.
  5. Mr D informed the Commissioner in April 2015 that:[192]
    “As per the financial statements for LST Trust previously provided, these show the following changes in issued units held by Mrs WYVW:
    Year ended 30 June 2010 $1,134,325 Issued units

    Year ended 30 June 2011 $489,325 Issued units

    This was a reduction of $645,000 as documented in the attached unit register, financial statements and Mrs WYVW’s bank account. These documents clearly showed the deposit related to a redemption of units and therefore is not income.”

  6. The Commissioner reached his decision having considered the following:
    • 422.1. there was a lack of appropriate records in accordance with the terms of the deed to demonstrate the actual intention of the parties and the true characterisation of the transaction. For example:
      1. no unit certificates, evidence of payment has been provided to demonstrate the acquisition of the units took place;
      2. no documents have been provided evidencing the units have been cancelled (Clause 8 of the LST Trust Deed provides that the units shall be cancelled as from the date they are redeemed);
      3. the LST Trust unit register supplied by the Applicant[193] showed the Applicant redeemed the units on 30 June 2011, but the $645,000 was deposited on 14 February 2011; and
      4. there are no contemporaneous records to evidence the Applicant’s request to redeem the units.
  7. The Commissioner also identified the following inconsistencies between some documents and information provided:[194]
    “I. The unit register provided by Mr D on 23 October 2014 showed that 1,989,000 units were redeemed in the 2009 income year. Mr G stated that there was no redemption of 1,989,000 units in the 2009 income year. Mr G also stated there were discrepancies in the financial records for the 2008 financial year whereby the Applicant was incorrectly credited with 3,024,325 units. This amount appears to have included units that were in fact held by Mr R, who contributed $2,090,000 to the purchase by way of acquisition of units.

    II. In the LST Trust’s unit register you supplied, the balance of your unit holding was 489,325 units as of 30 June 2011. This was not consistent with the LST Trust’s 2011 MYOB records as it debited an amount of $3,024,325 in GL 3-HZZA (Subscribed units) in the 2011 income year. This casts doubt the accuracy of the unit register of the LST Trust you supplied.

    III. The LST Trust’s 2011 MYOB showed on 14 February 2011, the $645,000 was recorded as a loan asset as follows:

    Debit – Account 1-CZZC ‘Loan to Mrs WYVW’ $645,000

    Credit – Account 1-AAAD ‘Westpac No 1’ $645,000

    IV. There was no CGT event declared in your 2011 income tax return.”

  8. In her statement the Applicant stated that:
    [491] “On 14 February 2011 as part of the transactions relating to the restructuring of the businesses discussed from paragraph 528, $645,000 was deposited into my bank account by Westpac.

    a. The deposits are listed in Part G.4 of the 2011 Worksheet.

    b. At Tab 151 is a copy of the LST 2011 MYOB records. I recorded this in account no 1-CZZC “Loan to Mrs WYVW” at page 2337 because it was received prior to the finalisation of the unit redemption.

    [492] At the end of Tax year 2011, this amount was allocated as payment for the redemption of units to that value in the LST Trust.

    [493] It was always intended that this payment would be for a redemption of units in the trust.

    a. It was identified as such in the transaction instructions prepared for Westpac as referred to in paragraph 529. The specific instruction is at T-510

    “Subscribed units – Mrs WYVW” at T-1149 records a debit reflecting an amount payable to me upon the redemption of units.

    c. At Tab 95 is a copy of the LST 2011 Financial Statements showing at page 1483 the reduction in the value of units held by me as a result of the redemption of units to $489,325 from the 30 June 2010 value of $1,134,325 (Tab 94 page 1474).

    [494] The $50,455.37 net capital gain from this redemption of units has been included in my calculation of my taxable income. The remainder of this payment is a return of subscribed capital and not taxable income”.

  9. There is no explanation for why the deposit was paid prior to the purported unit redemption.
  10. Again, there are insufficient records and inconsistent representations made about these transactions such that the Tribunal cannot be satisfied that the deposit was from proceeds from redemption of units in the LST Trust. No corroborating witness was called.

Finding

  1. The Tribunal is not satisfied, on the balance of probabilities, that the undeclared deposit of $645,000 deposit was from proceeds from redemption of units in the LST Trust to the Applicant. The $654,000 deposit, therefore, comprises part of the Applicant’s assessable income.

CONSIDERATION – 2012

  1. The issue in relation to the 2012 Year concerns whether certain expenses can be deducted from the Applicant’s assessable income. This is dealt with at paragraphs [543]-[612] below.

CONSIDERATION - 2013

The Total Amount Of $522,912.90 Described As A Distribution Of A Capital Gain & The Proceeds Of The Redemption Of Units In The LST Trust

  1. In the 2013 Year, the Applicant received bank deposits from LST totalling $522,912.90. In the Applicant’s 2013 ITR the Applicant only declared $71,309 as trust distributions from the LST Trust.
  2. The Applicant provided the following description against each deposit:[195]
  3. The Commissioner was not satisfied that the deposits were proceeds from loans and amended the 2013 ITR on the basis that the deposits from LST totalling $522,912.90 represented undeclared income.
  4. The Applicant contended that:
    • 432.1. there is no undeclared income because:
      1. she derived a capital gain of $159,348 during the year (resulting in a net taxable capital gain of $85,120 as declared in the 2013 ITR);
      2. $301,000 of the deposits were proceeds of the redemption of units held in LST. The following journal entries were recorded the LST’s 2013 Handisoft GL with a corresponding description “Redemption of units”:
      “Dr. GL. DBC0.01 Subscribed units – Mrs WYVW $301,000

      Cr. GL DZEC.01 Physical distribution $301,000”

  5. after the deductions of these amounts, the net distribution identified by the deposits above is $62,564 which is $8,745 less than the presently entitled income declared.
  6. A review of the LST 2013 Handisoft GL DZEC.01 discloses the deposits totalling $522,912.90
  7. The Commissioner does not accept those amounts represent a capital gain distribution made by the LST Trust and proceeds from redemption of units in the LST Trust for the following reasons:
    • 434.1. The net capital gain amount is unclear:
    “Based on the LST Trust’s 2013 CGT schedule, the net capital gain of $85,120 is calculated as follows:

    Capital gains – Real Estate $90,556

    Amount of capital gains from a trust (Including a managed fund) $159,348

    Total capital gain: $249,904

    Total CGT discount applied ($124,957)

    Small business active asset reduction ($39,837)

    Net capital gain $85,120.”

  • 434.2. The Applicant has not identified which deposits related to her alleged capital gain distribution;
  • 434.3. There are no contemporaneous documents such as unit certificates or payment direction to support the Applicant held the specified number of units in the LST Trust which entitled her to receive the alleged capital gain distribution from the LST Trust.
  • 434.4. In relation to the $301,000 described as “proceeds proceeded from the redemption of units in the LST Trust” there are no contemporaneous documents:
    1. such as unit certificates or payment direction to support the Applicant held the specified number of units in the LST Trust giving rise to the alleged redemption of units in the LST Trust;
    2. to support any agreement between the Applicant and LST that the proceeds from the alleged sale of units of $301,000 were to be credited in the Applicant’s loan account instead of providing physical payment;
  • 434.5. the records and explanations are inconsistent:
  1. Mr G stated that the Applicant redeemed 301,000 units in LST in the 2013 Year, which is consistent with the LST’s 2013 Handisoft GL DBCZ.01 (Subscribed units – Mrs WYVW) showing a redemption of 301,000 units. In the LST’s 2013 MYOB records, it shows that the Applicant redeemed 303,000 units, not 301,000. The LST Trust unit register shows that on 26 April 2013 the Applicant redeemed 303,000 units, resulting in a unitholding balance of 33,897 units as of 30 June 2013.[196] The Applicant says the unit register contained an error and it should have been recorded that 301,000 units were redeemed;
  2. in contrast to Mr G, Mr P advised that the Applicant redeemed units in the LST Trust for $495,316 on 8 May 2013.[197]
  3. In her statement the Applicant stated that:
    1. [633] “In the 2013 tax year I received deposits totalling $522,912.90 from the LST Trust. The deposits are listed at Part G.4 of the 2013 Worksheet:
    a. On 18 October 2012 I received a payment of $6,000 from the LST Trust. This payment was not recorded in MYOB, but I advised the accountants of this payment. It is recorded in the LST 2013 general ledger account DZEC.01 with the description “Transfer from WBC 2 – as advised WYVW”. I on-lent this amount to K WYVW.
    i. At T-1111 is a copy of the LST Trust 2013 General Ledger. Account DZEC.01 is at T-1115.

    b. On 31 January 2013 I received an amount of $11,596 from the LST Trust. This was to pay the stamp duty on the mortgage for the funding of the Moncur St Property. I recorded this in MYOB account 6-BAAE “Drawings Mrs WYVW”. It is also recorded in general ledger account DZEC.01 at T-1115.

    i. At Tab 189 is a copy of the LST Trust 2013 MYOB records. Account 6-BAAE is at page 2780.

    c. On 15 March 2013 I received the sum of $10,000 from the LST trust. I recorded this in LST MYOB account 1-CZZC “Loan to Mrs WYVW” at page 2775. It is also recorded in general ledger account DZEC.01 at T-1115

    d. On 8 May 2013 I received the sum of $495,316.90 from the LST trust. This payment was an advance on the anticipated distributions from the trust for that year and was sourced from the proceeds of the sale of (the Glebe property) by the trust that year. See from paragraph 596. I recorded this in MYOB account 6-BAAD “Distribution Mrs WYVW” at page 2780. It is also recorded in general ledger account DZEC.01 at T-1115.

  4. [634] The taxable component of these deposits is $89,398.16, consisting of:
    a. The trust distribution of $31,472. See paragraph [330].

    b. The net taxable capital gain of $39,837 from the sale of (the Glebe property) by the trust. See paragraph [596].

    c. The net taxable capital gain of $26,646.02 from the redemption of units in the LST trust. See paragraph [599].

  5. [635] The non-taxable component of these deposits is $424,957.88. This comprised:
    a. The non-taxable component of the capital proceeds, which totalled $393,864.98:
    i. $119,511 from the sale of (the Glebe Property) ($159,348 - $39,837)

    ii. $274,383.98 from the redemption of units ($301,000-26,646.02)

    b. The repayment of $31,092.90 against the amounts owed to me by the trust.

    i. At the beginning of the 2013 tax year, the trust owed me $286.75.

1. At T-1113 General ledger account DZZZ.01, my beneficiary account, shows this as the opening balance.

2. At Tab 169 is a copy of the LST Trust Financial Statements for 2013 showing at T-2560 a liability to me of $287 at the end of the 2012 financial year.

ii. On 17 May 2013 I deposited $31,000 in the LST bank account. At that time the LST Bank account was overdrawn by $30,689.14.

1. I recorded this in MYOB account 2-CZCA “Loan from Mrs WYVW” (Tab 189, page 2777).

2. This is transaction 881 of the Annotated Transactions (Tab 11, page 63)

iii. Account DZEC.01 also records two adjusting credits totalling $455.70.

iv. The total of the above amounts ($287.75 + 31,000 +455.70) is $31,742.45. This is $649.55 greater than the remaining $31,092.90 received by me, meaning that amount was not income.”

  1. There is no conclusive evidence the Applicant owned units in the LST Trust, and no evidence that processes for unit redemption were followed.
  2. The lack of sufficient records and inconsistent representations made about these transactions are such that the Tribunal cannot be satisfied that the deposit was from proceeds from redemption of units in MZA #1.
  3. These transactions relate to entities under the Applicant’s direction and under supervision and guidance of tax accountants and solicitors. It is reasonable to expect that documentation would have been prepared.
  4. No corroborating witnesses were called.

Finding

  1. The Tribunal is not satisfied, on the balance of probabilities, that the undeclared deposit of $522,912.90 deposit was from proceeds from redemption of units in MZA #1 to the Applicant. The $522,912.90 deposit, therefore, comprises part of the Applicant’s assessable income.

An Amount Of $79,631 Described As Payment Of UPE From The MZA #1

  1. In the 2013 Year, the Applicant received a bank deposit from MZA #1 of $79,631 on 12 November 2012. This amount was not disclosed in the 2013 ITR. The Applicant declared $0 as trust distribution from MZA #1.
  2. The Commissioner requested the Applicant explain the difference between the loan listed of $33,025 from MZA #2 and the $79,631 received from MZA #2 with the description “proceeds of loan”.[198]
  3. Mr D advised:
    “As at 30 June 2012, Mrs WYVW was owed $79,631 from the MZA Trust. This was repaid in the 2013 income year. Mrs WYVW also drew additional funds of $33,025 from MZA #2 in the 2013 year.”
  4. The Commissioner formed the view that the Applicant became presently entitled to the $79,631 deposit at the time it was deposited into her bank account given the Applicant did not provide any evidence of the existence of a loan, no interest was charged and there was no fixed term for the alleged loans.[199]
  5. At objection the Applicant contended the $79,631 deposited by MZA #1 was a payment of a UPE. The Applicant referred to MZA #1’s 2013 GL DZZZ.01 – Opening balance beneficiary showing the opening balance of the purported UPE was $79,631.07 as of 1 July 2012.
  6. The Commissioner contends that the deposit of $79,631 received on 12 November 2012 has been correctly assessed to the Applicant as assessable income in the 2013 income year because:
    • 446.1. there are no contemporaneous documents (such as unit certificates or bank statements) to support the purchase and subsequent sale of units. Clause 7 of the MZA Trust Deed requires unit certificates be issued to unitholders;
    • 446.2. documents and explanations are inconsistent:
      1. Mr G represented the $79,631 was a repayment of a UPE. By contrast Mr D represented as at 30 June 2013 there was no UPE;[200] and
      2. Mr G confirmed there was only one profit distribution in the period 1 July 2009 to 30 June 2013 (occurring in the 2011 income year).
  7. The Commissioner set out in its reasons for decision the profit distribution totalling to $274,360.30 (calculated based on the unit holding percentage as per the unit register rounded to 1 decimal place) made to the Applicant by MZA #1 in the 2011 income year:[201]
  1. Unit Holders
  1. Balance of units held
%
Distribution
  1. Mr WYVW Pty Ltd Super Fund
  1. 11,570
  1. 0.50%
$1,378.70
  1. Mrs WYVW
  1. 2,164,873
  1. 99.50%
  1. $274,360.30
  1. TOTAL
  1. 2,176,443
  1. 100%
  1. $275,739.00
  2. Contrary to the unit register, the actual income distributed to the unit holders as declared in MZA #1t’s 2011 original ITR was as follows:
  1. 2011 Income Year
  1. Mr WYVW Pty Ltd Super Fund ($)
Mrs WYVW ($)
Distribution
  1. Share of income non-primary production
  1. 250
  1. ($176,109)
  1. $0
  1. Net capital gain
  1. 225,798
  1. $0
  2. At the hearing the Applicant stated that:
    1. [636]...
    a. “On 12 November 2012 I received a deposit in my bank account of $79,631 from the MZA #1 Trust.

    i. I recorded this amount in MZA #1 MYOB account 2-CZIA “Loan –Mrs WYVW”.

    1. At Tab 170 is a copy of the MZA #1 2013 MYOB records. Account 2-BZIA is at page 2566.

    ii. This payment is also recorded in the general ledger account DZEC.01, my current-year transaction account.

    1. At T-1102 is a copy of the MZA #1 2013 general ledger. Account DZEC.01 is at T-1115”.
  3. The Commissioner submits:
    [266] “In her WS the Applicant describes this amount as an unpaid present entitlement (WS-638b, page 165) which was owing to her since 30 June 2011 (MM-2347, Tab 152; Account No 1-BZHZ). The balance derives from 3 entries:
  4. (1) an opening balance of $84,869 (credit balance – MM-2064, Tab 140 but cf MM-1287, Tab 72 as addressed under 4.4.5(f) casting doubt on the accuracy of this opening balance)
  5. (2) a debit entry of $464,000 (dealt with at heading 4.4.5(i) above, the character of which was not established) and
  6. (3) a credit entry of $458,762 for which no details are available.
    [267] The doubts surrounding each integer contribute to the unreliability of the character of the net balance amount. Having failed to provide evidence of the circumstances attending the payment of $79,631, the receipt remains a bare payment which the Applicant has not proved is not her assessable income.”
  7. The evidence given by the Applicant is merely a description of what one can see from a review of the accounting entries. The Tribunal repeats what it has already said about the conclusiveness of journal entries.
  8. The Applicant provides no explanation for the inconsistent submissions made by her representatives at different times. The inconsistencies in the explanations of the records demonstrates that the records, on their own are not sufficiently reliable.
  9. In Bendel and Commissioner of Taxation (Taxation) [2023] AATA 3074 the Tribunal said that:
    [2] “The typical manifestation of [an unpaid present] entitlement is a resolution to vest an entitlement to annual income in a beneficiary of a family trust without any further step being taken or event occurring to discharge the entitlement.”
  10. There is a lack of documentation or contemporaneous recognition (other than journal entries). Documents one may expect to have recorded an unpaid present entitlement include:
    • 454.1. minutes of trustee meetings;
    • 454.2. distribution resolutions; or
    • 454.3. loan agreements.
  11. No other witness was called to verify the Applicant’s claim.

Finding

  1. The Tribunal is not satisfied on the balance of probabilities that the deposit of $79,631 constitutes a repayment of prior year entitlements. The amount of $79,631 therefore comprises part of the Applicant’s assessable income.

The Total Amount Of $222,226 Described As Proceeds Of Loan From The TAT Trust

  1. In the 2013 Year, the Applicant received bank deposits from the TAT Trust of $222,226. This amount was not disclosed in the 2013 ITR. The Applicant declared $65,927 as trust distribution from the TAT Trust.
  2. Following the audit, the Commissioner amended the 2013 ITR on the basis that the difference of $156,299 ($222,226 deposits received less $65,927 income declared) represented undeclared income.
  3. The Applicant contends that:
    • 459.1. the distribution of $156,299 is comprised of a loan to her from the trust;
    • 459.2. she repaid the loan in the 2014 income year;
    • 459.3. in the 2014 income year, the Applicant’s loan liability to the TAT Trust was transferred to the SDT as part of the restructuring of the family trust arrangements; and
    • 459.4. payments were made by the Applicant to the SDT in June 2014 of $333,090.[202]
  4. The Applicant provided:
    • 460.1. an excerpt of a bank statement of SDT to evidence the repayment of the purported loan which extinguished the transferred liability.[203]
    • 460.2. an excerpt of the TAT Trust’s 2013 balance sheet which showed the amount owed by the Applicant was increased by $194,451 (from $226,674 to $424,215).
  5. The Applicant says the total loan amount owed should have been recorded as $219,215 and not $424,215 in the TAT Trust’s 2013 financial statements, and the amount owed to MZA #1 should have been recorded as an asset of $65,000 instead of a liability of $140,000.[204] The amount of $219,215 is comprised of:[205]

a. an opening balance of $229,674.26 (GL account DZZZ.01);[206]

b. movement during 2013 of $17,687.71 (See GL DZEC.01 – Physical distribution); and

c. a beneficiary distribution of $28,146.[207]

  1. The Applicant provided a revised financial statement of the TAT Trust for the 2013 income year and noted that these amended financial statements for the TAT Trust were consistent with the previously issued financial account for MZA #1.
  2. The Commissioner reached his decision having considered the following:
    • 463.1. there were no loan agreements, no interest charged, and no fixed term for the purported loans;
    • 463.2. there was no evidence supporting the existence of any loan; and
    • 463.3. following Weyers case (see paragraph [299] above), interest-free loans made to beneficiaries of a trust with no real expectation of repayment are more properly described as distributions.
  3. The Commissioner was not satisfied with the Applicant’s contentions because:
    • 464.1. there are no contemporaneous documents such as loan agreements or evidence to support that the parties agreed to transfer loan amounts.
  4. The Commissioner also identified that some documents and explanations provided are inconsistent:
    “Amounts from TAT Trust – loan amount:

    I. Mr D advised that you owed $424,215 to the TAT Trust as at 30 June 2013 and in your response dated 22 November 2017 at page 18, you provided an excerpt of the TAT Trust’s 2013 balance sheet showing that the amount owed by you was $424,215.[208]

    Further, the 2013 TAT Trust’s Handisoft GL DZZZ.01 shows that a balance of $229,674.26 was owed by you as at 30 June 2013.

    Amounts repaid

    II. Mr G advised that the full loan amount was repaid in full in the 2014 financial year. [209] He later advised that the loan was not repaid but reallocated to the SDT in 2014, where in June 2014 you made a payment of $333,090 to SDT which extinguished the transferred liability.[210] Mr G later advised that the amount repaid was actually $333,039, this was due to a typographic error.[211]

    III. You provided the SDT and your bank statement to show that you made the relevant payment as summarised below.[212]

SDT Westpac Flexi Account statement No. 43

Date
Amount
Description
9 June 2014
$220,000
Fnds Tfr from Mrs WYVW...

$113,039
Fnds Tfr Gift- Mrs WYVW 30-Jun
Total
$333,039

Your Westpac One Account statement No. 55

Date

$220,000

$113,039
